# ACTIVE BUG REGISTER

Authoritative high-level work queue as of 2026-08-17. No identifier was removed, reused, or renumbered.

Detailed history lives in [the original evidence archive](archive/2026-08-12-bug-register-history.md) and the
[complete pre-cleanup snapshot](archive/2026-08-17-bug-register-pre-cleanup-snapshot.md). Search those files and the
named spec/evidence before implementation. Old line numbers are hints only; relocate by symbol. This file contains
dispatch state, not full investigation narratives.

Status rules: **OPEN/READY** permits scoped work; **METAL** requires the named hardware check; **OWNER CALL** requires
a decision; **PARKED/RECORD ONLY** is not **dispatchable** without a new ruling. Closed and superseded records live in
the archives.

### Gate-reliability update — 2026-08-28

- **B206 — S2 CORRECTED / SELF-GATE PASSED / AWAITING QG:** [the B206/B138 deterministic-measurement design](superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-b206-b138-deterministic-board-measurement-design.md)
  independently reproduced the current defect. Two clean, same-source, in-place `gateway`
  builds measured **503220 vs 503204 flash** solely because they contained **2 vs 1**
  timestamp strings; only `.text` moved, by 16 B. Three consecutive one-stamp
  `heltec_mobile` builds reproduced exactly at **1329244 flash / 218916 RAM**, so the older
  one-stamp ±16 report remains an intermittent lead, not an established noise rule. S1
  centralized build identity. S2's fixed-identity runner then reproduced exact RAM,
  flash, loadable sections, object counts, payload hashes, source identity and toolchain
  state in two clean arms on each approved ABI, while leaving normal `.pio/` untouched.
  QG accepted those measurements as genuine but held closure: schema 1 recorded only CXX
  command/wrapper state, and its comparator-field test was not independently exhaustive.
  Schema 2 now records and compares CC, CXX and LINK separately, proves LINK aliases CXX
  on both real ABIs, and has three role-specific wrapper controls plus an independent
  61-leaf inventory with every field mutated one at a time. All 18 named tests pass and
  both corrected ABI pairs are exact. ✅ QG rechecked and PASSED that evidence 2026-08-28 — B206 CLOSED.
- **B138 — CLOSED 2026-08-27 / S1:** the specifically reproduced two-TU
  timestamp/literal-merging mechanism is removed. One read-only authority now feeds both
  banner and BLE output; both gateway incremental controls retain exactly one linked
  stamp, with zero RAM or flash movement on the two measured ABIs. B206's broader
  deterministic-runner qualification subsequently passed QG and closed on 2026-08-28;
  it did not reopen B138.
- **B253 — ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-28 / QG-PASSED / BUILD PROVENANCE:** untracked non-ignored files now produce `-dirty` (bytes-only porcelain, non-UTF-8-safe); every Git-failure arm ABORTS the board build with one bounded diagnostic (`nogit` unproducible, test-pinned); `$PROJECT_DIR` not CWD; derived 13×1 + native×0 hook coverage with structure-level controls; the S2 override untouched with `source=` visibility. 37/37 tests, probe 12/12 controls RED, both ABI pairs exact, the real `GIT_DIR=/nonexistent` board-build abort proven pre-compilation.
  [the B253 provenance design](superpowers/specs/2026-08-27-b253-untracked-build-provenance-design.md)
  records the isolated next slice. `tools/git_rev.py` uses
  `git diff --quiet HEAD`, which ignores untracked files. An untracked source selected by a
  board `build_src_filter` can therefore alter the image while its banner lacks `-dirty`.
  B206/B138 are now closed, satisfying the prerequisite. Fix B253 separately: include
  non-ignored untracked paths, make Git lookup/status failure loud for normal board builds,
  preserve B206's one fixed-identity override, and derive coverage across all effective board
  environments. The design explicitly corrects an impossible gate: new manifests compare
  exactly within the B253 A/B pair, while only payload/measurement fields—not the deliberately
  changed source snapshot—may be compared to historical B206 manifests.

The AUTOMATED GATE is green and independently re-verified, which is a different and lesser claim. **Gate re-verified by the QA-gate, not relayed:** native **1680/83432 → 1704/83745 / 0 failed** (binary run; wrapper's false "0 test cases" reproduced), 14 native mutation controls RED, `probe_prov_tx` 6/6 + 6 controls RED, census unchanged at 174/178/178 with `-Wswitch` 0, four-step corpus inert (`b77cfd3d`, keystone `9868cad3`/269905 read from the anchor table, recompile control fired), `git diff -- lib/` **empty**, `sizeof(Node)` **221880** tripwire compiled. ⚠ **`tools/probe_board_ui` W12/W14-W20 were RE-PINNED** because the write now routes through `ICfgStore::save`; the new `CFG_ROUTED_SITES` term is **earned, not free** — QA-gate confirmed it is gated on the route being present (`run.sh:412`), so it cannot absorb a missing notify. ⛔⛔ **OWNER CALL: the team console path's stack peak grew ~224-312 B** (ARM `handle_team` frame 536→848 with the callees inlined; xtensa deepest chain 704→928), the largest single item being the **spec-mandated** 256-B `NodeConfig` role-projection copy (§3.3 forbids hand-deriving the role). **No metal `stackhw` reading exists** — the coder correctly touched no hardware while the [[B196]] soak runs. This path has recorded stack-overflow history. ★ **Six behaviour changes to expect:** `team 0` + any PHY arg now **refuses** (was a silent no-op) · a static node's `team new freq=…` now **honours** the PHY and reports bad tokens instead of ignoring them · a truly-unchanged same-team request reports **no change** with zero save/apply · two refusals swapped order (key error now reported before role) · the incomplete-PHY check reads the **candidate + build floor**, not live state · the private key no longer survives in a static scratch buffer. ⛔ **UNCOMMITTED (D4).** ⛔ Does **not** close [[B193]]: fake-store tests cannot establish physical NV atomicity. **Metal residue → bench Part 27.** ★★★ **PART 27 RUN 2026-08-18 ON `fc89e14`: SEVEN PASS, THREE NOT-RUN, FOUR NEW DEFECTS FOUND.** ✅ **27.8 (the round-3 live-divergence fix) PASSES on metal** — with the record and the request identical and only the radio diverged, the verb APPLIED (`↻ rx-freq → 869.4625` + `> team PHY:`), where the pre-fix behaviour would have discarded it as `no change`. ✅ **27.5 is the decisive one:** a same-team re-key applied while the node was keyless, preserved `team_local_id=220`, and **BOTH the id and the key survived a power-cycle** ⇒ the candidate did NOT persist `team_local_id = 0` on a same-membership request, which is precisely the defect design v2 corrected. ✅ Also 27.1 (stack: `stackhw` ≥ **4408 B free of an 8 KB task = 54 % headroom**, 4.4× the floor — ⓘ the 1504 B the verb consumed covers the WHOLE chain incl. LittleFS and X25519, ⛔ not attributable to this slice's 888 B frame), 27.2, 27.3, 27.4, 27.9. ⛔ **NOT RUN, each with a reason: 27.6** (needs a static node; both bench nodes are mobiles and `team 0` does not demote), **27.7** (no way to provoke a real NV write failure), **27.10** (the state is unreachable by construction). ⛔⛔ **The run found FOUR defects — [[B209]] [[B210]] [[B211]] [[B212]] — and exposed THREE of Part 27's own checks as unable to test what they claimed** (a field absent on the target platform, an expectation contradicting the check's own point, an unreachable premise); all corrected in place. ⇒ **the metal half of this row is DISCHARGED apart from the three not-run items; final closure waits on [[B209]] per the owner's execution order.** ⛔⛔ **QG HOLD 2026-08-17 (round 3) — ONE BEHAVIOURAL BLOCKER: `no_change` COMPARES ONLY AGAINST NV AND IGNORES LIVE DIVERGENCE, so an explicitly requested change can be SILENTLY DISCARDED.** `src/firmware_provisioning_service.h:526` is `!membership_changed && !differs`, where `differs` is candidate-vs-**record** only, and `:397` documents the live blind spot rather than closing it. **Concrete failure:** `mobile register freq=…` mutates the live config and radio **without** updating the record (`adopt_mobile_phy`, `lib/core/node.cpp:869`) ⇒ a later `team <current> freq=<the persisted value>` yields a candidate identical to NV ⇒ `no_change` ⇒ **the explicitly requested PHY is never applied and the radio stays on the divergent live PHY.** The same holds for an explicitly supplied key when the LIVE key is absent or different. ⚠ **The QA-gate had scoped this out as an owner choice; QG overrode that, correctly — an explicit command must not be discarded because NV already contains its requested value.** ⇒ **REQUIRED RULE: `no_change` = candidate equals the persisted record **AND** membership already matches **AND** every EXPLICITLY REQUESTED live domain already matches.** ★ **And it is cheap — the accessors already exist, verified by the QA-gate: `Node::active_freq_mhz()` (`lib/core/node.h:388`), `Node::active_bw_hz()` (`:375`), `cfg.layers[0].routing_sf` / `allowed_sf_bitmap` (`lib/core/node_carriers.h:33-34`), and `team_channel_key_present()` / `team_channel_pub()` / `team_channel_priv()` (`node.h:224-226`).** ⛔ The earlier claim that *"`NodeConfig` has no `freq_mhz`"* was true only of the top-level struct and is not a reason to defer. Controlled cases owed: **persisted PHY equals request but live PHY differs ⇒ `applied` with EXACTLY ONE PHY application**; **persisted key equals request but live key differs/absent ⇒ `applied` with EXACTLY ONE key installation.** ✅ **CLOSED — QA-gate verified at the code.** The rule is now `!membership_changed && !differs && live_phy_matches(...) && live_key_matches(...)` (`src/firmware_provisioning_service.h:604-606`), with `live_phy_matches` (`:325`, all four PHY fields) and `live_key_matches` (`:339`). `ProvSnapshot` gained four live-PHY scalars plus **POINTERS** to the live keypair (`:112-125`) — ★ pointers deliberately, so no second copy of the team secret enters the console frame, and `team_channel_pub()/priv()` already return `nullptr` for "no key" so the absent case needs no flag. Filled by the **adapter** at `src/firmware_config.cpp:1353-1358` from `active_freq_mhz()` / `active_bw_hz()` / `layers[0].{routing_sf,allowed_sf_bitmap}` / `team_channel_p*()` — ⛔ no new core accessor, no `NodeConfig` copy, no second `Blob`. ★★ **The service remains PURE — QA-gate measured it with comments stripped: `g_node` / `g_hal` / `g_persist_team_local_id` / `LORA_FREQ` / `LORA_BW` all ZERO** (the raw hits are prose); pinned by probe **S12** with control **C15** RED. ★ **A finding the coder added beyond the brief: the key comparison includes the PRIVATE half**, because `team_channel_key_load` installs verbatim — a record whose halves disagree would otherwise yield a node whose pub matches while its priv cannot decrypt. Control **M-E** (compare the public half only) is RED. **Gate re-verified by the QA-gate:** native **1710/83827 → 1713/83937 / 0 failed** (binary run), `probe_prov_tx` **12/12 + 16 controls RED** (⚠ **C10 went UNUSABLE at match count 0 when the phase-2 signature moved and was RE-PINNED — reported, not silently passed**, which is the standing rule for a moved mutation target), `probe_board_ui` unchanged, census 174/178/178 `-Wswitch` 0, corpus four-step inert with the recompile control firing, `git diff -- lib/` **empty**. **Six envs SUCCESS, RAM +0 EVERYWHERE**, flash +0/+0/+288/+252/+272/+228 — both gateway envs byte-identical because `handle_team` compiles out at `MR_N_LAYERS == 2`. ⚠ **Stack moved again and rides the ACCEPTED metal qualification, not a new design change: ARM `handle_team` 856 → 888 (+32, the extended snapshot); xtensa deepest chain 976 → 928 (−48).** ★ **New operator-visible behaviour: a `team <current>` naming a PHY or key the RADIO/NODE does not already hold is now APPLIED** (one coalesced save, one live application, still zero airtime) — previously discarded as `no change`. ⓘ **[[B206]]'s mechanism was measured here:** two `__DATE__` TUs landing in different seconds produce two un-deduplicated stamp strings, +16/+32 flash (`gateway` 481620 vs 481588; `xiao_sx1262` 533588 vs 533572) ⇒ **flash arms must be single-stamp on BOTH sides.** ⛔⛔ **QG HOLD 2026-08-17 — THREE IMPLEMENTATION DEFECTS, all verified at the code by the QA-gate:** ① ★★ **`no_change` is computed from the REQUEST SHAPE, not from the candidate.** `src/firmware_provisioning_service.h:405` reads `!membership_changed && key_action == preserve && !phy.present` ⇒ **(a)** re-supplying the ALREADY-ACTIVE PHY reports `applied`, runs live PHY handling and notifies the UI; **(b)** supplying the IDENTICAL existing key reports `applied` and reinstalls it; **(c)** ⛔ the dangerous one — a **role-projection-only** candidate correction (same team, no key, no PHY) is classified `no_change` and therefore **SILENTLY DISCARDED**. ⇒ derive no-change from the **candidate's actual differences against the stored record**, tracked while staging fields (⛔ not via a second `Blob` copy — see ③), with controls for identical-PHY, identical-key and role-only. ② ★★ **A NEWLY ASSIGNED `team_local_id` MAY NEVER REACH NV.** The candidate persists `team_local_id = 0` (`:325`) through `ICfgStore::save`, which ⛔ **does not update `g_persist_team_local_id`** — that tracker is written ONLY at boot restore (`src/fw_main.cpp:797`) and after a successful `persist_cfg_if_needed` save (`:1042`). So the tracker still holds the OLD team's id while NV holds 0; if DAD then happens to pick **the same numeric id the old team used**, `team_changed` at `src/fw_main.cpp:1030` is FALSE, nothing is persisted, **NV stays 0, and the next boot needlessly re-DADs** — the very outcome design v2 corrected. ⇒ after a successful save, **sync the tracker to the saved value (0) before DAD**, or force the post-DAD persist; ⚠ the sync belongs in the **adapter/caller**, not the pure service (which cannot see `fw_main`'s globals). Add a deterministic old-id-reuse test. ③ ⛔ **A COMMENT ASSERTS A PROPERTY THE CODE DOES NOT HAVE** — `src/firmware_provisioning_service.h:299-300` claims the 256-B `NodeConfig` projection copy *"dies at the closing brace, never alongside the Blob AND the plan AND a keypair"*, but `apply_team` (`:431`) holds the plan and the candidate live across that block, so **they do coexist**. ⇒ QG recommends **splitting role projection into an earlier phase whose copy is destroyed before the `TeamPlan` and `Blob` exist, then RE-MEASURING** the stack. ★★ **STACK — RESOLVED AS A MEASUREMENT, AND THE PREMISE WAS REFUTED (2026-08-17). ⛔ WITHDRAWN IN PLACE:** this read *"The stack increase is NOT approved as-is (ARM 536→848 B, xtensa 704→928 B on a path where `stackhw` once fell to 72 B)"*. **The 256-B `NodeConfig` copy is NOT the cost and never was:** ARM `handle_team` measures **856 B with the projection first, 856 B with the plan+Blob first, and 856 B with the copy DELETED OUTRIGHT** — `-Ofast` scalarises it, because `role_enforce` touches exactly three fields (`lib/core/node_role.h:79-85`). ⇒ **the growth is the TYPED CARRIERS** (`TeamRequest` 136 + `TeamPlan` 128 + `ProvResult` 64 vs the old frame's 64 B of `tk_pub`/`tk_priv`); pre-slice ARM was **536**, and the QG-requested split bought **+8 B** (`sizeof(TeamProjection)`), not a reduction. ⓘ xtensa deepest chain is **976** (304 → 592 → `apply_phy` 80); the earlier **928** took a shorter leg and understated the peak by 48 B. ⛔ **The "72 B" history is WITHDRAWN as an explanation of present risk** — it predates the dedicated 8 KB mesh-task fix that moved this work off the 4 KB Arduino loop stack, so it does not describe current headroom. ⇒ ★ **ACCEPTED AS A METAL QUALIFICATION rather than a code blocker (QG 2026-08-17): bench Part 27 must be run on the resulting firmware IMMEDIATELY.** Reducing the frame would mean changing the seam's carriers (drop `TeamPlan::phy` ~40 B, let the plan reference the request's key buffers ~64 B) — a design change, ⛔ not authorised. ④ **Output overclaim:** `src/firmware_config.cpp:1184` says *"no flash was changed"* on the failure arm — ⛔ **not established**, since a backend may fail after a partial physical write ([[B193]]). ⇒ say only **"persistence failed; no live state was applied and no airtime was spent"**. |
| B209 | ✅ **FIXED + QG PASSED 2026-08-18 — ⛔ UNCOMMITTED, metal-verify pending (bench 27.11)** | **`team new` / `team <id>` with `freq=… sf=… bw=…` silently authorises static-home attachment even when `mobile_autoregister=false`.** Metal reproduction: after `team new freq=869.4625 sf=7 bw=125`, `mobile status` reports the contradictory but diagnostic tuple `autoregister:false`, `home_desired:true`, `attachment:"seeking"`, followed by repeated outbound mobile J `DISCOVER`s. Root cause is exact: `DeviceProvLive::apply_phy()` (`src/firmware_config.cpp:1099-1108`) calls `Node::mobile_register_phy()`, whose documented contract (`lib/core/node.h:647-649`) is **retune + `mobile_request_home_service()` + immediate DISCOVER**. The durable retries are therefore expected after the wrong authorisation is created; this is not a retry-timer defect. **Cross-spec conflict:** the B207 transaction spec records the old call as “retunes and kicks the FSM” (§1 line 40), but its normative seam says `apply_phy` is the PHY operation and `fire_dad` is the airtime operation (§3.1-3.2). The mobile-home spec is explicit: auto-OFF stays dormant until explicit `mobile register`, Heltec has no hidden registration, and required gate 9 says auto-OFF emits nothing; `test_node_join.cpp` already pins an auto-OFF team member performing team-DAD with zero DISCOVERs. **Required correction:** make provisioning `apply_phy` use a clearly named retune-only core seam built on the existing `adopt_mobile_phy()` mechanism; it must preserve `home_desired`, attachment state and registration timers. Keep `mobile_register_phy()` unchanged for the explicit console/app registration verb. `fire_dad()` remains the separate, last team-plane airtime operation. Correct the provisioning spec's inherited wording in the same slice. Pin: (1) auto-OFF + team create/join with PHY ⇒ team-DAD/local id succeeds, `home_desired=false`, `dormant`, zero mobile DISCOVER; (2) explicit `mobile register freq=…` still enters `seeking` and DISCOVERs; (3) an already-authorised manual/auto session remains authorised across a team PHY apply without a second hidden authorisation; (4) mutation routing the adapter back through `mobile_register_phy()` turns the regression RED. ⓘ Metal workaround: `mobile unregister` stops the loop without leaving the team or clearing the team-local identity. ★★★ **FIX LANDED 2026-08-18.** ⓘ **ATTRIBUTION, CORRECTED — this row first read "verified at the code by the QA-gate" in a way that could be taken for a QG pass, and it was written BEFORE any QG review existed.** The honest split: **(a)** the QA-gate re-verified the code shape and re-ran native + `probe_prov_tx` on 2026-08-18 **before** review — that is a coder-claim check, not an approval; **(b)** ★ **an independent QG review PASSED the slice later the same day** with no implementation blocker, confirming the separation (provisioning retunes without authorising; the explicit verb keeps its behaviour; an existing authorised session is preserved without an extra discovery attempt) and requiring only the two documentation corrections applied here. New public retune-only seam **`Node::mobile_retune_phy`** (`lib/core/node.h:663`, inside the `#if MR_FEAT_MOBILE` block at `:635`, delegating to `adopt_mobile_phy` **and nothing else**; ⛔ no non-mobile `#else` stub invented — its neighbours have none). `DeviceProvLive::apply_phy` (`src/firmware_config.cpp:1114`) points at it; **`mobile_register_phy` (`node.h:648`) is PROVABLY untouched — `git diff -- lib/core/node.h` is +14 insertions and ZERO deletions** — and `handle_mobile` (`:1435`) still calls it, so the explicit verb keeps authorising. `fire_dad` untouched. ⓘ The drifted seam comment at `src/firmware_provisioning_service.h:284` — the wording that let the defect in — was corrected. ★★ **PIN 3's VACUITY WAS CONFIRMED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT ARGUED: under the seam mutation `home_desired` and `attach_state` BOTH PASSED; only `count_armed(kMobileDiscoverTimerId)` discriminated.** ⇒ QG's insistence on a timer-arm count was load-bearing — without it pin 3 would have been the eleventh instrument in this arc that could not fail. **Controls:** the mutation reddens pins 1 and 3 (5 assertions) while **pin 2 — the unchanged positive control — stays GREEN**, exactly as the corrected control rule requires; `C16`/`C17` redden probe `S13` (C17 covers deleting the retune outright, so banning the wrong verb cannot pass by removing the right one). **Gate re-verified by the QA-gate:** native **1713/83937 → 1716/83966 / 0 failed** (binary run), `probe_prov_tx` **13/13 + 18 controls RED**, census 174/178/178 `-Wswitch` 0, `sizeof(Node)` **221880**, `kCap` 91, `git diff -- lib/` = `node.h` only. ★ **Corpus — a STRONGER result than ordinary inertness:** the header edit forced a genuine **34-object rebuild** and `lus` still reproduced **`b77cfd3d`** byte-for-byte (an uncalled inline emits no code), with s18 `9868cad3`/269905 behind it; anchor table untouched. **Six envs: RAM +0 everywhere**, flash 0/0/−128/−20/−8/−16 — the four `MR_N_LAYERS<2` envs SHRINK, the expected sign now that `apply_phy` no longer inlines the authorisation and the timer arm. ⇒ **metal residue is bench 27.11 only.** |

## Protocol, routing, and message correctness

`-Wswitch` covers the enum, and `node.h:550` warns never to rewrite these as if-chains). ⛔ **Pins: (a)** with `autoregister=0` and no session, `cfg` must NOT say "scanning"; **(b)** with an explicit `mobile register` outstanding it must say `seeking`; **(c)** ★ **in `claiming` it must NOT say `REGISTERED`** (the false positive); **(d)** `recovering` must render, not fall into a default; **(e)** `attached` with no home id must report an inconsistency. ⓘ **Coverage is structural** — `src/firmware_commands.cpp` is compiled by neither the native suite nor the simulator; Next tick pulls fresh rows (tombstone-filtered, `inbox.h:132-137`) but the model is now CLEAN ⇒ `FrameGate::step` answers idle ⇒ **no repaint ever comes**: the artefact persists until an unrelated event dirties the model. The `:1641` comment solved the `UiState` half of exactly this staleness and missed the SNAPSHOT half. Meanwhile the model's identity-tracked cursor correctly follows the NEIGHBOUR captured before the delete (`note_inbox_neighbour`) ⇒ the stale row's text and the row's real identity disagree — the mis-open the owner observed. ★ **Fix shapes:** (a) a serviced inbox MUTATION leaves the model DIRTY FOR ONE MORE FRAME (a needs-fresh-snapshot latch — the next tick's fresh pull then repaints; cheapest, pure, mutation-testable "dirty survives the stale frame"); or (b) re-fill the snapshot's inbox rows after the drain, before the freeze (zero-artefact but re-orders the tick — check the `:1641` rationale and the frozen-chrome rule). ⓘ Either way the regression case is host-drivable: erase → next frame's rows must not contain the erased identity. ⚠ Same class as [[B231]]'s ripple — one dispatch can carry both list fixes. ⓘ **OWNER OBSERVATION (same session): deleting the LAST message of the list refreshed CORRECTLY.** Consistent with the mechanism, as a HYPOTHESIS the fixer must confirm: the per-tick `sync_inbox_cursor` re-anchors by IDENTITY against the fresh snapshot — when the last row was deleted the remembered pick/neighbour identity no longer exists ⇒ the identity tracker notices, sets `dirty`, and the NEXT frame repaints from fresh rows; a middle-row delete leaves the cursor on a still-valid neighbour ⇒ nothing re-dirties and the stale frame stands. ⇒ the artefact hides exactly when the post-delete cursor identity is valid — the regression suite must drive BOTH arms (delete-middle: stale today; delete-last: refreshed today; after the fix BOTH refresh, and the delete-last case guards against the fix regressing the working arm). ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-20, QG PASS — shape (a):** a success-only one-shot `_inbox_rows_stale` latch raised on the `erased` arm, consumed by the next `on_tick` into `dirty` ⇒ the following tick's existing fresh pull repaints; **`pulls == ticks` COUNTED** in all three cases (zero extra store traffic; shape (b) would read one more). Delete-middle reproduced RED against the UNFIXED tree first (the frozen frame still carried the row 2.4 s later; no ninth frame ever opened); delete-last guarded (M95). Mutations **M94/M95/M96** RED at match count 1; the probe's `v3` arm gained the on-glass *"the list catches up with NO further press"* check (hand-verified non-vacuous). ⚠ **THE HYPOTHESIS WAS CONFIRMED IN OUTCOME BUT ITS MECHANISM CORRECTED (measured):** delete-LAST refreshes ⛔ NOT because the identity vanishes — the fallback neighbour stays valid — but because the predecessor lands at a DIFFERENT INDEX, and `sync_inbox_cursor`'s **index-move branch** (`_st.cursor != i`) is what re-dirties; a middle delete leaves the neighbour at the SAME index, so nothing notices. Both facts asserted in cases, not narrated. **MEASURED:** native **1835/86848/0** · 96 RED / 0 unusable. Panel recheck = bench **Part 31**. | B234 | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-20 — ALREADY SATISFIED (owner re-ruled: UI-ONLY was the intent; the row overstated the ruling)** | ★ **LEAVING OR REPLACING A NON-ZERO TEAM REQUIRES EXPLICIT CONFIRMATION.** This is not an OLED-only feature: today UI-15's CREATE/JOIN flows have BACK-default confirmations, but the serial/BLE console can execute the same membership change directly. The reliable rule is deliberately stronger than testing `rt_team_count() > 0`: that table is transient and its absence cannot prove the mobile is the team's only member—especially after connectivity is lost. Therefore guard every operation whose committed candidate changes `current_team_id != 0` to a different id or zero. This includes `team new`, `team <different-id>`, `team 0`, and `leave`; `factory_reset confirm` already satisfies the shape. Same-team re-key/PHY/no-change does not leave and must not be gated. **Required shape:** one shared provisioning/command predicate before entropy, NV write, live apply or airtime; typed request carries explicit confirmation; console/BLE use a stateless terminal `confirm` token (never echo a command containing private key material); OLED may satisfy the same typed flag only after its existing BACK-default confirmation. First unconfirmed attempt refuses loudly and changes nothing. Tests must cover alone/no-route as well as known-peer states, proving that route expiry cannot remove the guard, plus every exit verb and same-team negative controls. ⛔⛔ **CORRECTED IN PLACE + CLOSED 2026-08-20 (owner, reported form): the ruling above OVERSTATES the owner's intent — B234 was meant UI-ONLY, and the cross-transport console/BLE expansion is WITHDRAWN** (a partial implementation was dispatched on the row's wording, stopped, and surgically reverted the same day; the tree carries none of it). **On the UI the guard ALREADY EXISTS and is measured:** slice 5's `create_confirm` opens with **BACK selected** and shows the **`REPLACES <fp>`** warning whenever a create would displace a non-zero team (QG-passed; metal-checked, walkthrough step 30 — BACK proven zero-transaction on air), and **no OLED flow for `team 0`/`leave` exists at all**. ⇒ nothing to build; **closed as satisfied by §UI-15 slice 5.** ⓘ If a console/BLE `confirm` guard is ever wanted, that is a NEW ruling and a new row — ⛔ do not resurrect this one's cross-transport text as authority. |

| B238 | **OPEN / LOW / PROBE-HARNESS CLOCK-WRAP TRAP / found §UI-17 S5, 2026-08-21 — documented in-source, workaround in place** | ⚠ **THE FEATURE PROBE'S `walk_to_slot` STEPS THE CLOCK BACKWARDS ~90 ms PER WALK, WHICH `ArduinoClock::accumulate_millis_wrap` READS AS A MILLIS WRAP AND ANSWERS WITH +2^32 ms.** (`leave_list` returns after its own `paint(t)`; the caller paints at the same `t`.) A long probe run crosses **137 such phantom epochs**, so any node-side timestamp taken BEFORE a walk is ~49 days old AFTER it — S5's first P19 cut failed exactly that way (labels dropped past `team_key_of_id`'s 48 h gate, `id 84 … 49d` on the panel). ⓘ **Harness-only; shipped firmware is untouched** (real `millis()` never steps back). **Workaround, in place and written out in-source at P19: walk FIRST, stamp SECOND, never walk between a stamp and the frame.** The shared helper was deliberately NOT changed mid-slice (C1). **CLOSE BY:** a small harness slice making the probe clock monotonic across `walk_to_slot` (or wrap-proofing the helper), with a control proving a stamp survives a walk — until then every new probe phase must follow the stated discipline. |
| B239 | ✅ **CLOSED IN-SLICE 2026-08-22 (§UI-17 S8 — found by the coder's own probe BEFORE landing) / DEADLINE-WRAP REVIVAL CLASS** | ⚠ **A WRAP-SAFE `now < deadline` ON A DEADLINE THAT IS NEVER CLEARED REVIVES ~24.8 DAYS AFTER IT EXPIRED** (once `now` runs 2^31 ms past it, the signed-wrap comparison reads the stale deadline as future again — for the NEXT ~24.8 days). S8's first cut armed `_msg_wake_until_ms` and never cleared it ⇒ a node that received ONE message would stop blanking 24.8 days later — the F-10 power regression arriving from the CLOCK instead of the traffic. ⓘ `hold_active` carries the same bare comparison and is immune ONLY because its `retained` term clears — **the immunity is the CLEARING TERM, not the comparison**. Found because the probe's backwards time jump made a stale deadline look live (P20a failed); **fixed by BOUNDING the window** (`left != 0 && left <= kBlankMs`), pinned by a 37-days-later case + mutation **S17**, with a fourth uptime base covering the one blank the bound alone cannot see. ★ **THE STANDING LESSON for every deadline in this codebase: a wrap-safe comparison over a deadline needs either a CLEARING term or a WINDOW BOUND — audit any new `now < deadline` for which one it has.** |
| B240 | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-22 (same day) / QG-PASSED after one 3-blocker round · ⛔ UNCOMMITTED, metal = the drafted keyring bench part** | ⛔⛔ **THE SEALED `team grantkey` RECEIVE SIDE ADOPTS THE KEY TO RAM ONLY — A REBOOT LOSES IT.** `team_key_grant_receive` (`lib/core`) adopts live and pushes; the firmware's push handler (`src/fw_main.cpp:1470-1474`) only PRINTS; the sole writers of `Blob::team_ch_*` are reached exclusively via `seed_blob_from_live` on OTHER verbs' save paths — **no path persists a key that arrived by grant.** ⇒ a member who received the team key over the air can read the encrypted channel until the first power-cycle, then silently cannot — the worst shape for a hiking safety device (works at the trailhead, dead at the summit), and design §3.6.4 point 6's "durable adoption" is FALSE today. ⓘ Pre-existing (the mobile-arc key-forward slice landed the transport, not the persistence); found by the UI-16 spec's F-6 inventory. **PLANNED FIX — RE-RULED BY OWNER 2026-08-22: a dedicated `/mrteams` KEYRING, not a small `src/` patch** — a new indexed NV record (`TeamKeyBlob`: magic/version/count + 4 × `{team_id, team_ch_pub, team_ch_priv, reserved}` — four entries matching the four join profiles, ~296 B flash, no permanent Node RAM growth), with the owner's policy set (one record per team_id · atomic replace · identical-material writes nothing · a FULL keyring fails LOUDLY, never silently evicts a secret · factory reset erases it · no labels · secret buffers wiped · boot install only on exact active-team match) and the RETENTION/ACTIVATION rule: keys are RETAINED on leave but ⛔ NEVER silently reactivated by mere knowledge of the public team_id — `/mrcfg` gains an active-binding fact and re-joining a known team offers `SAVED KEY FOUND` / explicit `USE SAVED KEY` (BACK default). Persistence-FIRST on grant receive (only `saved` reaches the UI; a failed save reports `TEAM KEY ACTIVE` / `NOT SAVED — LOST ON REBOOT`). Owed its own metal power-cut part. Sequenced FIRST in the UI-16 arc; ⛔ still must not wait if UI-16 defers. ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-22, QG PASS:** the `/mrteams` keyring landed as ruled (K1+K2 — 296-byte record measured, four-state read, counted zero-write coalescing, loud-full refusal through the whole transaction, retained-never-reactivated, `/mrcfg` v24 active binding — an NV version, not wire). ⚠ **QG's first gate found THREE boot/transaction holes, all one family — the keyring's verdict was not the sole authority over the live key:** the legacy `/mrcfg` boot install ran FIRST (a failed restore left the old key live) · a FAILED same-team re-key became effective after reboot (keyring written before the failed `/mrcfg` save, binding still active) · membership was missing from the exact match (a stale binding could install another team's key). **Fixed structurally:** the legacy install REMOVED (`/mrcfg`'s pub demoted to the COMMITTED WITNESS), a **FIVE-TERM restore predicate** (active · membership==binding · record present · keyring pub == committed pub · derived pub == stored priv — no new NV field), and every refusing arm exits through ONE `refuse()` funnel that clears the live key. Both boot arms proven RED against the pre-fix code first. **MEASURED:** native 1967/88323/0 · `teamkeyring` 25 RED / `provservice` 10 / `devicenv` 19, all 0 unusable · both probes green (147 + 46 controls). The B240 falsifier: native P05 + the bench part's power-cycle step. |

This proves the defect is not restricted to overlong names: a short successful copy also leaves its following byte untouched, so stale non-zero tail bytes become a visible suffix; a name ≥ 15 additionally guarantees the existing out-of-bounds-read shape. `label_from_hash` (`src/firmware_ui.cpp:437-438`) only `snprintf`-overwrites on a **0** return, so its `%s`/`strlen` consumers receive no termination guarantee. ⓘ N4's invite-member projection terminates explicitly (`src/firmware_ui.cpp:744-755`) and says why; the shared helper remains the unfixed authority. **FIX SHAPE:** terminate in ONE place — preferably make `peer_name_find` guarantee NUL termination whenever `out != nullptr && cap > 0`, with the returned length excluding the terminator; otherwise terminate centrally in `label_from_hash`. Do not add renderer-specific clearing. **CLOSE BY:** a small dedicated slice (a `lib/core` contract change carries corpus obligations). |

| B341 (S5-Q1) | **FIXED IN SLICE 5 (fold-in) / QA-VERIFIED 2026-09-07 — closes at the Slice 5 commit** | **Fix landed by the Slice 5 coder and verified by QA's independent gate:** `admin_runtime_boot` installs each valid half independently (root + its ten prepared epochs when `/mradmid` is ok; the ACL image + a prepared epoch per occupied row when `/mracl` is ok with an owner; a bad half installs ITS cleared half); readiness stays the recomputed conjunction. Executed on both instruments: native `§radmin-5/R16` (sequence B), `R17` (sequence D), `R18` (bad half clears its own half) through a `RealRuntime` applying plans via the production `remote_session_install`; inbox-verbs `S5-7a..f` / `S5-8a..f` across the real `admin_stores_boot_report_console()` on the real `g_node`; `radmin5runtime` T15 (the pre-fix conjunction-gated boot) and T16 (a half-fix) both redden. QA gate figures: native 2825/119784/0, ACCEPT arm 180/30, union 516 RED / 1 unusable (the unusable is B342, pre-existing). Original finding kept below. — `src/firmware_admin_runtime.h` `admin_runtime_boot` commits the CLEARED image (`clear_root` + ACL reset) whenever `admin_provisioning_ready` is false, i.e. whenever EITHER record is bad or the ACL has no owner — it discards the VALID half too. The verbs then install only their own half: `AclService::commit_` prepares `set_acl` (never the root), `AdminIdService::mint_` prepares `set_root` (never the ACL image). ⇒ two ordinary sequences print a durable success line while the running node stays `disabled` until the next reboot: (B) `admin-id generate` → reboot → `acl add owner <key>` prints `> acl added slot=0 role=owner` but `root_present` is 0 and `remote_session_accepting` is false; (D) ACL valid with an owner, root record corrupt → reboot → `admin-id reset confirm` succeeds but `acl_occupied` is 0 and the node is not accepting. QA REPRODUCED both on native against the REAL `RemoteSessionState` driven by `remote_session_install` (a runtime that applies plans exactly as `Node::admin_session_commit` does): `QA-S5-REPRO-B` fails at `remote_session_accepting` = false / `root_present` = 0; `QA-S5-REPRO-D` fails at `acl_occupied` = 0 / accepting = false; every durable assertion (result ok, record saved, epochs minted) passes. The shipped `§radmin-5/R14` and inbox-verbs `S5-1a..S5-6b` never cross the boot with only ONE record valid, which is why both stayed green. This is the recurring 'a success that isn't' shape (a console `ok` describing state the running node does not hold), and no console line exists after boot that would reveal it. **CLOSE BY (fold-in, Slice 5):** the boot installs EACH valid half independently (the root with its ten prepared epochs when the id record is ok; the ACL image with a prepared epoch per occupied row when the ACL record is ok), readiness stays the recomputed conjunction, and a bad half still installs ITS cleared half (no stale live ACL, no stale root). Property to test natively and in the inbox-verbs probe: after any sequence of durable successes, the running readiness/slots equal what a fresh boot on the same medium installs — the two reproduction sequences become executed cases (`R14` extended, plus two probe rows), and `admin_runtime_boot`'s `draws`/`commits` expectations in `R14` are re-derived, not accommodated. The design §6.4 rule stays: no implicit provisioning, no fallback key, no invented owner. |

| B369 (S7b1-P5) | **CLOSED — INDEPENDENT QA PASS 2026-09-08** (folded into brief rev 2) | Coder preflight: a request waiting for transcript capacity loses its ingress body at the 300 s expiry (`remote_session.cpp:320-332`) while its seen row stays `admitted`; a later exact retry classifies as `replay_transcript` with nothing to replay (pinned at `test_remote_session.cpp:805`). **Resolution:** ingress expiry of a NEVER-EXECUTED request releases its seen row too (no at-most-once obligation exists; [[B332]] retention is about executed rows); a retry re-admits fresh; the Slice 5 case is rewritten with the old obligation visible. **Follow-through (brief rev 3):** the ACK is consumed WITHOUT an ingress reservation so it can free capacity while an owner waits in the CONTROL row; every fixture pinning the old retention (I4/C5/P3/E2/E5 in `test_remote_session.cpp`, N9 in `test_node_remote_session.cpp`) is rewritten, capacity proofs rebuilt from retained executed rows. **Closure:** final source and the full native/probe/mutation gate independently verified; Slice 7b-1 QA evidence §§2–4. |
| B370 (S7b1-P6) | **CLOSED — INDEPENDENT QA PASS 2026-09-08** (folded into brief rev 2) | Coder preflight: remote `acl set`/`acl remove` (owner class) would reach `AclService::set`/`remove` through `acl_verb` with the DEFAULT `AclActor{}` (`firmware_admin_verbs.h:374, :390` — correct for the local USB caller, which has no slot), so the service's self-slot protection (`firmware_admin_acl.h:318, :343`; design §6.6) would not apply and a remote owner could demote or remove its OWN authenticating slot, invalidating its session mid-handler. **Resolution:** `CommandContext` gains `acl_slot` (filled from the authenticated seen row; none for local callers); `acl_verb` passes `AclActor{true, slot}` from the active context; probe rows prove `self_slot` remotely and byte-identical local behaviour. **Follow-through (brief rev 3):** the promised `refused` terminal for a slot emptied after admission is WITHDRAWN — normal invalidation clears the slot's seen/ingress/transcript rows and the retired session can carry no reply; pending work is abandoned (no dispatch, no reply); a stale-view refusal is at most a labelled synthetic case. **Closure:** final source and the full native/probe/mutation gate independently verified; Slice 7b-1 QA evidence §§2–4. |

| B374 (S7b1-I2) | **CLOSED — INDEPENDENT QA PASS 2026-09-08** (folded into brief revs 5/6) | Coder checkpoint: the brief's terminal mapping said an encode/sealing failure after a truthful result becomes `internal_error`; the response nonce is deterministic per `(key, ctl, request_id, response_seq)` (`lib/core/remote_codec.h:285`), so replacing a frozen terminal's plaintext at the same seq is NONCE REUSE (design §10 case 4). Reproduced synthetically by the coder; production already preserved the transcript. **Resolution:** a completed transcript is immutable; a send-time seal failure keeps the frozen result and cursor, increments a new `response_seal_failure` counter, and (rev-6 clarification) retries that pending frame on the next eligible main-loop pass; an authenticated exact request retry restarts at seq0. `internal_error` is a completion-time execution/staging result only (§8.9), never a send-time replacement. **Independent takeover QA, 2026-09-08:** fresh isolated rebuild of the existing synthetic case = 1 case / 32 assertions / 0 failed; separate QA immutability/replay proof = 1 / 128 / 0. No production nonce-reuse vulnerability demonstrated. At that takeover checkpoint the SEND wrapper still classified seal refusal as enqueue failure; its counter/emit and Node-path proof were pending, so the focused result was not slice closure. Evidence: `docs/superpowers/evidence/2026-09-08-radmin-slice7b1-qa-takeover.md` §§3–4. **Final closure:** the sender now separates seal/enqueue counters and events, with no enqueue on seal refusal. QA independently executed the labelled SYNTHETIC real-Node proof (142 assertions): full retained-state comparison, nonzero cursor preservation, saturation, automatic next-pass recovery and authenticated seq0 replay. New transcript/executor/sender batteries total 44/44 RED; full gate PASS. Evidence: `docs/superpowers/evidence/2026-09-08-radmin-slice7b1-qa-gate.md` §§2–4. |

| B392 (S7b3-Q1) | **RULED R-RA-38 — SOFTWARE-COMPLETE in 7b-3 (`6086152`, QA 2026-09-16); the 8a warning LANDED (8a+8c); the 8b carrier LANDED (8b QA 2026-09-18) — OPEN for Part 57b metal only, NOW RUNNABLE** | **A remotely activated `prep-restart` makes the target remotely UNREACHABLE until a LOCAL restart (USB/BLE console `reboot`, hardware reset or power-cycle).** Verified at `f993191`: `handle_prep_restart` sets `g_halted` (`src/fw_main.cpp:438`); the main loop skips the WHOLE operating block while halted — RX drain, timers, TX and `mrfw::remote_executor_service_once()` (`:1759`) all sit inside `if (!g_halted) {` (`:1372` … `:1803`); only the WDT feed, USB/BLE console and the no-sleep latch (`:1832`) keep running. | B403 (S8AC-P1) | **CLOSED — CONTRACT PRECISION, folded into 8a+8c brief revision 2 (QA 2026-09-17); no code change, not a bypass** | The 8a+8c brief §§3/7 requires invalid Ed25519 conversions to refuse while §1 says to mirror the target `derive_base` with the same primitives/refusals. At `6086152` (production inputs unchanged at `e3a5fa0`), `identity.cpp::ed_pub_to_x25519` is void; the vendored conversion computes `(1+y)/(1-y)` without Edwards point validation, and `remote_ecdh_shared` rejects only all-zero X25519 shared results. **46 labelled synthetic characterization checks** calling the unchanged actual `remote_session.cpp::derive_base` show valid identity success, low-order refusal with unchanged output, and acceptance/key publication for all four off-curve encodings `y=2,7,8,11`; separate finite-field checks prove each has no Edwards x. This is a validation-contract mismatch, not a demonstrated authentication bypass. **CLOSE BY:** QA specifies the controller's checked input-validation boundary and invalid/off-curve/noncanonical/low-order controls; any target/shared-helper behavior change needs an explicit fence decision because target/codec changes are currently out of scope. Do not silently weaken the brief or copy an unchecked conversion as its proof. No production fix. Receipt and reproduction: [8a+8c preflight](superpowers/evidence/2026-09-17-radmin-slice8ac.md). **Resolution:** design §7.1's "reject an invalid peer conversion" is satisfied at this source by the X25519-level refusals (`remote_ecdh_shared` all-zero = every low-order point; `remote_kdf_base` all-zero input) plus the stores' encoding checks; the vendored Monocypher exports no Ed25519 point decompression, so an on-curve check cannot be added without new field arithmetic. It is not a security gap: only operator-installed keys (`acl add`, `admin-target add`) or exact ACL matches reach the conversion, and an off-curve encoding cannot authenticate because no seed produces it (an attacker who can install keys is already trusted). The controller mirrors `derive_base` exactly; the four off-curve encodings stay a labelled characterization control. Install-time canonical/on-curve validation is parked as **B404**. Design §7.1 carries the precision note. |

| B408 (S8B-QA-1) | **CLOSED — 8b INDEPENDENT QA PASS 2026-09-18 (found by QA while authoring the 8b brief at `c07b77f`)** | **The controller's `-a` capacity bound reads the wrong ring.** `Node::remote_client_correlation_free()` (`lib/core/node.h`) counts free `_deleg_acks` rows — the HOME-side delegated-flight correlation ring, populated only by `deleg_ack_put` when a home re-originates for a hosted mobile. A client-only mobile never hosts, so on every product mobile the count is a constant 8 and `remote_client_start`'s `correlation_full` refusal (`remote_client.cpp`, `correlation >= in.correlation_free`) is unreachable (the pending table has four rows). The ring a mobile's `-a` send actually occupies is `_pending_e2e_acks` (`cap_pending_e2e_acks` = 8): `enqueue_data` arms it for a `MOBILE_SEND` wrapper (wildcard key, 300-s tier) and `delegate_send_layer` for the cross-layer wrapper — and a FULL ring at arm time is not refused there: it emits `e2e_ack_untracked_ring_full` and the send flies untracked (the console's `send`/`send_layer` verbs pre-refuse with `e2e_ack_ring_full()`, `node.cpp`; the controller does not). With a real carrier an `-a` request could therefore fly with no ACK/timeout tracking and its row would never report `acked` or `ack_timeout`. Inert in 8a+8c (the stub carrier sends nothing); design §15's "one of the mobile's eight shared delegated-correlation rows" names the wrong ring for the mobile side. **CLOSE BY (8b, brief §3/§4):** re-point `remote_client_correlation_free()` to the free `_pending_e2e_acks` rows and make the carrier pre-check `e2e_ack_ring_full()` before an `-a` submit (U1: the console's predicate); controls remove each half; design §15 wording corrected at the 8b landing. **Resolution (8b, QA 2026-09-18):** `remote_client_correlation_free()` counts free `_pending_e2e_acks` rows; `Node::remote_client_submit` pre-checks `e2e_ack_ring_full()` before an `-a` submit and returns the typed `RemoteClientSend::correlation_full`; the coder's 30-check reproduction and the `radmin8client`/`radmin8brx` controls for both halves are RED in QA's fresh union. [QA gate](superpowers/evidence/2026-09-18-radmin-slice8b-qa-gate.md). |

| B409 (S8B-P1) | **CLOSED — FOLDED INTO 8b BRIEF REVISION 3 (QA 2026-09-18)** | Revision-2 8b at `c07b77f` requires emit-free core hooks immediately before three existing push sites, immediate USB/BLE output through the request transport, and no observation state. | B410 (S8B-P2) | **CLOSED — FOLDED INTO 8b BRIEF REVISION 3 (QA 2026-09-18)** | Brief §3 falsely says existing `send_ready` passes `e2e_ack` only for execute. A plain `full` keeps its meaning (TX-queue refusal, `radio_enqueue_failed`, counter incremented); ring-full is never inferred from an ACK-requesting enqueue failure. The rev-3 §1 claim that the interface was final was QA's; one enum value is the amendment. |

| B415 (S8B-QA-2) | **PARKED / LOW / PRE-EXISTING — registered by QA 2026-09-18 while folding B413; not 8b's** | **The E2E pending-ack ring clears a wildcard entry on the counter alone.** `Node::e2e_ack_clear` (`lib/core/node_mac.cpp`) iterates `_pending_e2e_acks` in ring order and, for an entry armed with `key == 0` (every delegated `MOBILE_SEND` wrapper and every hosted-mobile last mile — `enqueue_data`'s `delegated` arm), treats a matching `ctr` as a hit regardless of the acker. Counters are allocated per destination (`Node::next_ctr(dst)`), so a delegated send (counter space = the home) and a direct `-a` send to a known target (counter space = that target) can hold the same numeric counter at once; whichever E2E ACK arrives first can then clear the OTHER entry, giving the direct send a false `e2e_ack_timeout` and the delegated one a false `send_e2e_acked` (or vice versa). Needs both flights outstanding with equal counters — rare, but structurally reachable on any registered mobile. Not created by 8b: the controller's observation matcher (brief rev 5 §4) keys on counter plus kind-specific identity and inherits exactly this residual, documented there; observations are diagnostic, never terminals. **CLOSE BY (its own core slice, owner-ruled):** give wildcard entries a second discriminator (e.g. the acked frame's `DST_HASH`/origin for the delegated case, or a per-mobile counter space for wrappers) and a control that two equal-counter flights resolve to their own entries; measure any `PendingE2eAck` growth on the three ABIs. Not dispatchable without a ruling. |
